    i am so glad y'all liked chapter 5! i've been doing my best to reply to comments more 🙌 cw: mentions of blood and torture See the end of the chapter for more notes January 5th, 2017 | 09:01 A.M.

    Gunshots. Screams. The gurgling of a throat filling with blood.

    The smell of pine needles and wet earth permeated the air. It had snowed not too long ago— or had it? If it had, why did this place smell so much like summer rains and warm nights? Another breath, another gurgling scream.

    But no, that wasn’t right, was it?

    Looking at her hands, she noticed the bow and arrow in her hands.

    Right. She was on a hunt.

    “Wake up, Jack.” Turning to her left, Kate found herself face-to-face with her mother.

    “What?” Kate asked, looking around to make sense of it. The forest was bright and airy. The smell of pine needles and wet earth permeated the air. The summer rains and warm nights colored everything around them—or were they? Then why was there snow on the ground where the canopy wasn’t thick enough to hold it back?

    “I said: Pay attention, darling,” Eleanor said, holding her hunting rifle close to her body, the muzzle aimed at the forest floor. Her hair looked the same as it always did, but she was dressed all wrong for the forest. “We’re close. I can feel it.”

    “Close?” Kate asked, staring at her mother’s red dress. It flowed across the forest floor. “The wolf,” Eleanor said, lifting her rifle and aiming at something in the distance that Kate couldn’t see.

    The smell of pine needles and wet earth permeated the air. Bright sunlight illuminated the forest, making it impossible for anything to hide. What was her mother pointing at?

    “Mom?” Kate asked, bringing her hands to her face. Where had her arrow and her bow gone?

    Snarling and howling and a piercing pain of long, gnarly teeth. Her side burned with fire as the wolf’s teeth dug into her. But did it hurt? Was the pain there? Even as she was tossed to the side, she barely felt flinched.

    Black fur and bloody teeth.

    Blue eyes.

    “Look what you have done,” Eleanor said, holding her rifle close to her body, the muzzle aimed at the forest floor. Her hair looked the same as it always did, but she was dressed all wrong for the forest. “Look what you have done.”

    The smell of pine needles and wet earth permeated the air. Blood dripped from the leaves, landing on her mother. There were no stains; she was already dressed in blood.

    She couldn’t get up. Her legs wouldn’t move; her arms were glued to the wet forest floor. Black fur and bloody teeth.

    Blue eyes. Her eyes.

    In the distance, screams. Gunshots. Blood gurgling in a throat desperate for air. Or was that wrong, too? Did it snow or rain? Was it day or night? Summer or winter? There shouldn’t have been anyone else around. There shouldn’t have been anyone else in the forest.

    Whose blood was in her mouth?

    She was cold – she was never cold. Flexing her fingers, she marveled at the slowness of her own body. The weakness that permeated her bones and dug its claws into her arms and legs brought with it a question: What happened? What happened? What happened?

    A soft voice spoke to her, pulling her further away from the forest.

    “Suddenly, the night was ablaze with flashes of light as gunfire erupted across the water,” the woman said, hushed tones and gentle words informing Kate of what was going on. No wonder the earth was wet if there was water close by. Kate could almost see it out in the distance. Behind her mother. Behind the wolf.

    Squeezing her eyes shut even tighter, Kate tried to bring herself back. What did she remember—what had she been doing prior to this moment? She was cold, but there was a blanket draped over her. She was cold, but someone’s hand was on her shoulder.

    Furrowing her brows, Kate took a deep breath.

    “From out of the darkness a shadow raced along the sand, crouched low but clearly visible, and vulnerable, in the merciless light of the moon,” the woman said again, the warmth of her voice seeping into Kate’s body, warming her from the inside. Only—this was wrong, wasn’t it? She could not feel granules of sand beneath her hands. The smell of pine needles and wet earth permeated the air—wet earth. No sand. There was no ocean here—there was snow.

    “No,” Kate grumbled, repeating her mental refrain. This was all wrong. There had been no sand in her bite wound. There shouldn’t have been anyone else around. Her mother was the only witness to the bite. Her mother was the only one who saw her change.

    “No?” the woman asked, accompanying her question with the tender touch of fingers on Kate’s forehead. The fingers were warm, and Kate turned her head to seek more of them—

    more connection—more attention. Her moon. Her moon.

    “Nuh-uh,” Kate said, unsure why she was under a blanket when she could clearly see her mother’s bloody dress and the wolf in the forest. And who was stroking her hair if not her mother? Who was chuckling in her ear?

    “Wake up, Kate Bishop,” the woman said, pulling at a memory—this is not what we discussed.

    “M’sorry, mom,” Kate whispered, turning her head to the side. “I wasn’t careful.”

    But her mother didn’t say anything. Why didn’t she say anything? And where was the wolf?

    Where were the gunshots—the blood? The smell of—the smell of—there were no pine needles here. Here, the smell of cotton sheets and a familiar human smell permeated the air. “You are never careful,” the woman said, voice heavy with something Kate could not name.

    “But I am not your mother. You are having a bad dream.” The hand moved from Kate’s head to her shoulder, softly squeezing and shaking her. The image of her mother faded—in her mind, Kate lingered on her face—but she couldn’t quite make out the expression.

    “I’m sorry,” Kate said again, seconds before she opened her eyes and found herself face to face with— “Yelena?”

    “You are very stubborn,” Yelena said, closing Safe Harbor and putting it on the bed. “I will say this once and only once,” Yelena continued, placing both of her palms on Kate’s shoulders. “You do not go after hunters, Kate Bishop. No hunters for you!”

    “What?” Kate asked, furrowing her brows. “What are you talking about?” Now that she was awake, Kate couldn’t help but focus on the weakness in her bones. When she tried to sit up, her side erupted with pain, and a whimper slipped from between her lips. “What happened?” Yelena’s expression softened.

    “You do not remember?” she asked, sitting back on the bed with her ankles crossed and her knee wedged against Kate’s thigh. She was dressed in a light-gray pair of baggy sweatpants and a neon-blue blouse that was mostly see-through, revealing the black sports bra beneath. Yelena’s hair was undone, hanging in gentle waves around her face. Earrings twinkled in the sunshine pouring in from the window—one was red. As it dangled from Yelena’s earlobe like a drop of blood, a memory shot through Kate’s brain.

    She could almost smell it. Natasha’s blood. Heavy in her nostrils.

    More pain lanced through Kate as she tried to get up a second time, face paling at the pain that seared through her. “Fuck,” Kate grunted, digging her hands into the mattress and blanket. “Is Natasha okay? Is she hurt?”

    “Yes, and also no,” Yelena said quickly, sitting up to put both of her hands on Kate’s shoulders to push her back against the pillows. The well-meaning gesture only served to make Kate more anxious and frantic for movement. “Yes, she is okay. No, she is not hurt. You are the only one that is hurt.” Wriggling under Yelena’s hands, Kate looked around, expecting to see her mother in a bloody dress or Natasha’s dying body. “Kate Bishop!”

    Yelena kept her left hand on Kate’s shoulder before grabbing Kate’s cheeks with her thumb and the rest of her right hand. Squeezing Kate’s face, Yelena established eye contact between them. “Natasha is alive and well. She stepped out to make tea. So—” Yelena’s expression would have seemed terrifying to anyone else. “Will you rip your stitches over it?”

    “No?” Kate asked, staring at those green eyes that never failed to make her feel better.

    “I am not sure I believe you,” Yelena said, leaning down and lifting her eyebrows.

    “Yelena—” Kate said, the name sounding odd because of her jaw’s limited range of motion.

    When the eyebrows rose even higher, Kate relaxed her shoulders and took a deep breath. “I won’t rip my stitches. But in my defense,” Kate continued, rolling her eyes, “I didn’t even know I had them. Maybe you could, I don’t know, cut me some slack? Just a bit?”

    Releasing Kate’s face, Yelena leaned back and placed her hands in her lap while Kate flexed her jaw. Slipping her arms out from under the blanket, Kate rubbed at her face before running her fingers over her hair, barely remembering the gentle touches along her forehead. Had that been in the dream, or had it really happened?

    “What do you remember?” Yelena asked, smoothing her face back into perfect neutrality.

    “I…” Kate started, placing her palm over her eyes. What was the last thing she remembered? The smell of pine needles and blood hung heavy around her foggy memories, but there was nothing else. “I remember being at the farmhouse and talking about the mission.” The day before they left the farmhouse, Kate stayed awake for hours after going to bed. “Why did we get up at the ass crack of dawn?” She’d been tired and keyed up at the same time—always a dangerous combination for a young werewolf.

    “The early bird catches the worm,” Yelena said, her words poking at Kate’s brain.

    “More like, The early widow steps on her enemies,” a voice said from the doorway, prompting Kate to drop her arm and try sitting up again. The tray in Natasha’s hands held two cups and a slightly misshapen teapot. There were no visible wounds on her, but Kate didn’t have long to check because before she could start groaning in pain again, Yelena’s palms were pressing her back down.

    “Kate Bishop! What did we just agree to!”

    “C’mon,” Kate whined, watching helplessly as Natasha rounded the bed, disappearing from Kate’s view behind Yelena. There was something else—something else. The smell of blood and the sound of someone gasping in pain. Her dream swam before her eyes. A reality?

    “Now I can’t even say hi?”

    “You can say hi without hurting yourself,” Yelena insisted, patting Kate’s head with the flat of her palm.

    “Looking good, Jack,” Natasha said, coming back into view without the tray. There was nothing in her movement that indicated she was hurt, and yet... “Being on the verge of death really suits you.” Sitting down next to Kate on the bed, she leaned forward to give Kate a quick and painless hug. It was over too quickly for Kate to react, especially with Yelena still holding her down, but maybe that was for the best. “Welcome back to the land of the living.” “No,” Yelena said when she spotted the confused twist of Kate’s open mouth. “You did not die. We did not bring you back. Relax.” Natasha’s claps against Kate’s bicep were hard but not hard enough to cause pain. The fact that Kate felt them so acutely worried her regardless. “Go back to what you remember so we can fill you in on everything else.”

    With a slow exhale, Kate closed her eyes and furrowed her brows. Yelena and Natasha smelled familiar, even in this unfamiliar environment. They wouldn’t be returning to the farmhouse anytime soon— “I remember us driving for a long time. I remember falling asleep on the way.” Natasha had been in the driver’s seat while Yelena gave her mildly snide directions. Their bickering had been a soothing backdrop to Kate’s worried mind. “I don’t remember getting there.” Opening her eyes, Kate looked in between the two sisters. “Did it work? The trick with the phone?”

    “What do you think?” Natasha replied, her smirk kicking at some of the rocks that obscured Kate’s view of her own memories. Maybe she remembered climbing the fir tree to retrieve her duffle bag. Maybe she remembered handing Natasha her phone. Maybe she remembered Natasha’s smirk when she saw Kate’s purple phone case with the Avengers logo on the back. “You were right.”

    “I knew it! I told you!” Kate said, lifting her hand a little too fast to point at Natasha and reaping the uncomfortable results. “But I thought we agreed I’d stay in the car?” Looking down the length of her body and the blanket that covered it, Kate watched as Yelena slowly removed her hands. “Why am I hurt?”

    “Because you’re stupid,” Yelena said before sliding off the bed and picking up the teapot. While she poured, Kate looked at Natasha and raised one of her brows. In response, Natasha only shrugged and got to her feet as well.

    “I’ll check on our friends,” Natasha offered, rounding the bed and stopping next to Yelena. She whispered something to her, but Kate’s wolf was too incapacitated to hear what she was saying. When she stopped by the door, Natasha looked once more at Kate. “Not to be embarrassingly sincere or anything, but I’m glad you’re finally awake.”

    “Thanks,” Kate said, wondering just how much she’d missed and how badly she was injured for Natasha to say something like that. Watching Natasha’s retreating form through the slightly ajar door, Kate moved her right hand back under her blanket in search of her wound. “Do you want tea?” Yelena asked, startling Kate enough to stop her unqualified exploring. “You should eat something, too.” Without waiting for Kate’s reply, Yelena filled the second cup. “If you’d been out another day, I would have hooked you up to an IV—no matter what Natasha had to say about it.”

    “Tell me what happened,” Kate said, carefully slipping further up the bed by leveraging her palms against the mattress. Within an instant, Yelena was by her side. Putting down the cup of hot tea, she helped Kate sit up against the pillows. “I know what a silver bullet feels like, Yelena. How did I get shot?”

    “The plan worked perfectly,” Yelena said, picking up a glass of water from the nightstand and handing it to Kate. “They tracked your phone to the clearing, and they fell for the howling trick.” Sitting down on the side of the bed, Yelena pulled her right thigh onto the mattress and grabbed her ankle. Her red earring continued to glitter as it swung back and forth, tugging at Kate’s brain. Whose blood did she keep seeing? “We took them down.”

    “And I didn’t stay in the car?” Kate groaned, slapping her left hand to her forehead and almost dropping the glass of water all over herself with the other. She couldn’t imagine ignoring Natasha and Yelena’s legitimate warnings. The hunters would have been prepared to fight a werewolf—leaving the car to jump into the fray would have been reckless. Unless…

    “Are you hurt? Is that why—”

    “No,” Yelena said, removing the glass from Kate’s uncoordinated fingers and putting it back on the nightstand. “I wore a bulletproof vest, remember?” Yelena lifted her blue blouse and showed Kate the right side of her upper abdomen. There was nothing there except for the slightest bit of green-and-yellow splotching. “Good as new.”

    “I don’t remember that…” Kate whispered, wanting desperately to reach out and touch the discoloration on Yelena’s skin. The bruise had probably looked worse immediately after the ambush—sometimes Kate forgot that she wasn’t the only one who healed fast. “Is that why I left the car? Because you got shot?”

    Dropping her blouse, Yelena shrugged and shook her head.

    “We miscalculated,” she admitted, grabbing the edge of Kate’s blanket before slowly folding it down to expose Kate’s flannel-covered stomach. For the first time since she was turned, Kate shivered as the air of a room moved against her now unprotected body. “There were seventeen of them when we started shooting.” Gently, Yelena undid the three lowest buttons of Kate’s flannel. Under different circumstances, Kate might have blushed or stuttered about it, but Yelena’s face was predictably stony instead of salacious. “Natasha knocked out Kelly and Trask like we discussed, but…” The bandage that came into focus couldn’t entirely hide the black veins that protruded from underneath.

    Silver poisoning.

    Kate stared, fingers twitching at her sides as she held herself back from ripping off the thick gauze pad and revealing how horrible her side truly looked.

    “Natasha and I were in the middle of gathering up the corpses when the rest of their party arrived,” Yelena said, carefully placing her hand on Kate’s lower abdomen. Her thumb landed on one of the black veins, following it carefully so as not to touch the bandage. It didn’t hurt —somehow, it brought a relief Kate couldn’t name. She was alive. She would be strong again. Her wolf was hurt and dormant now—separated from the full moon for several more days—but it would howl and run once all this was over.

    “The rest of the party?” Kate asked, watching Yelena’s thumb move back and forth.

    Furrowing her brows deeper, Kate briefly closed her eyes. There was a hand—a hand reaching out—a hand missing her by mere inches—This is not what we agreed to.

    “Kelly and Trask travel with an entourage, yes?” Yelena asked, even though they both knew the answer to her question. “They did not bring one of their boys. We missed this. It was a mistake that should not have happened.”

    Opening her eyes, Kate spotted the tail-end of a guilty expression crossing Yelena’s features. Carefully, to avoid jostling her own wound, Kate covered Yelena’s fingers on her stomach. “Next time, we’ll just use the crystal ball,” Kate said, putting as much cheer into her voice as she possibly could.

    The guilt turned into a scowl.

    “You believe this not to be our mistake, but it is,” Yelena insisted, removing her hand from under Kate’s before getting to her feet. “We made note of their hierarchy structure, yet we did not wait for a more opportune time or to see if there were stragglers or late arrivals.”

    “Yelena,” Kate said, reaching out to clasp her friend’s wrist but missing it by mere inches as Yelena started pacing the floor. “C’mon! You said you were done with the ambush by the time the other group arrived! How long should you have waited? Until they were right up against your hiding place?”

    When Yelena didn’t reply, Kate pushed herself up further in a desperate effort to leave the bed and join her friend. She couldn’t quite swallow her whimper of pain when her silver-

    poisoned wound screamed in agony. Her leg was about halfway out of the bed when Yelena returned, her hands on Kate’s shoulders and her face drawn into a frown.

    “Kate Bishop,” Yelena threatened, seconds before Kate threw caution to the wind and wrapped her arms around Yelena’s back. It wasn’t an embrace. Considering the fact that Yelena was holding her down and glaring at her with all the passion of a thousand suns, Kate’s noodle arms were hardly strong enough to keep her in place. “What are you doing?”

    “I’m trying to show you that I’m totally okay and that you can stop beating yourself up over something that isn’t even your fault!” Kate’s hands softly patted Yelena’s back through her see-through blouse. For once, it was Yelena who felt warm to the touch. Their faces were close together—close enough for Kate to feel Yelena’s angry exhales on her face. Her friend’s green eyes were shrouded in emotions that Kate had no names for. “Remember when you said you would do anything to protect my freedom? Remember when you said that people die and that it’s nothing to be scared of?” Kate tugged ineffectually at Yelena, hoping to loosen her hold, but Yelena didn’t budge. “You can tell me why I left the car; you can tell me why I’m hurt.”

    “I shut off my comms!” Yelena blurted out, fingernails digging into Kate’s flannel. “I didn’t…” Yelena’s eyes dropped to the side, her brows and mouth twisting into a mess of uneven lines. “You were very upset after the Red Room attacked us.” Slowly removing her hand from Kate’s left shoulder, Yelena poked the skin above Kate’s heart. “I did not want you to hear—or to see—I did not want you to be so upset again about what we have to do. It is enough that Natasha and I will see their faces in our dreams. It is enough. It should have been enough.”

    “Okay…” Kate said gently, slackening her grip as Yelena made to sit down. A warmth that had nothing to do with body temperature spread through Kate’s veins at her friend’s admission. There was something about the way Yelena cared—something Kate had never received from anyone else. “You wanted to protect me, so you turned off communication to talk to Natasha without me overhearing?” Purposely keeping her tone soft and unthreatening, Kate grabbed Yelena’s hand and held it gently against her sternum. “And then?”

    “Then our hubris caught up with us.” Shaking her head, Yelena scowled and rolled her eyes. “And you left the car because you’d seen their heat signatures on the monitors. When we didn’t respond, or you couldn’t reach us, you decided to shift and chase after us.”

    “That seems fair!” Kate said, feeling a pout start at the corners of her own lips. She could almost see herself in the car, staring at a tablet and yelling into her earpiece. She didn’t want to imagine her own terror. Even now, hurt as she was, she would make the same decision again. “Why are you mad at me, then?”

    “Because we decided that you would stay in the car!” Yelena insisted, mouth turning into the perfect frown. She seemed on the verge of pulling away again, so Kate held her wrist and arm even tighter to her. “We decided.”

    “You can’t be mad at me for wanting to protect you!” Kate said, unable to hide her petulant undertone.

    “I can blame you for taking a bullet for me that would have been stopped by my bulletproof vest!” Yelena exploded, her hands going to the front of Kate’s flannel and grabbing it tightly. She didn’t jostle Kate in the slightest, but this time, it wasn’t because Kate was an immovable werewolf with super strength. No. This time, Yelena didn’t pull her close—instead, she leaned in furiously. “Death is inevitable, Kate Bishop, but you do not have to chase it.” “Well, neither do you!” Kate replied, pout now in full effect. Growing up as a hunter had not prepared her for this. Being gay in New York City had not prepared her for this. Being a werewolf had not prepared her for this. Yelena’s affection was a force—and her fear of losing Kate was as awe-inspiring as it was infuriating for its double standard. “You said I could fight for you if I can’t fight for myself. And I want to. I want to fight for you! I keep telling you that!”

    “I did not—” Yelena took a deep breath and closed her eyes, her fingers pressing harder against Kate’s chest. It didn’t hurt—all it did was remind Kate of how close they were and how much she didn’t want it to be under these circumstances. “When I said that—”

    “I would take a bullet for you again!” Kate said, interrupting Yelena while grabbing her biceps and squeezing them as best as she could in her weakened state. Her heart juddered erratically in her chest—her wolf was dormant, but Kate could feel its mounting presence. Yelena and Natasha were not just precious to her. “You saved my life on that highway. You can’t stop me from returning the favor. You protect me—I protect you.”

    When Yelena opened her eyes, Kate’s rambling mouth clacked shut. There was something dark and dangerous behind those eyes—something that—something—her heart throbbed thickly as her wolf continued to wake. Why did she suddenly feel the pull of the moon?

    “What happened in that forest will not happen again,” Yelena said, her voice deathly quiet. “You can’t know that, Yelena,” Kate whispered, doing her best not to make a face while the persistent ache in her side started to pulse with renewed anger. Her wolf strained inside her ribcage, stretching against the slumber that the silver poisoning had forced on it. The next full moon was well over a week away, yet a silver bullet alone should not have been enough to do such lasting damage. It must have been coated with liquid silver to poison her system from the inside out.

    “Yes, I can!” Yelena snarled, baring her teeth at Kate as she pressed her harder against the pillows.

    Kate’s side burst open with blinding pain as her wolf returned to her. Finally unable to hide it, Kate squeezed shut her eyes and screwed up her face, her hands trembling on Yelena’s biceps. A whimper ripped from her throat as her whole body went rigid.

    Immediately, Yelena relaxed her grip, her voice bouncing around in Kate’s brain.

    “What is happening?”

    “I don’t know,” Kate gasped, her hands clenching on Yelena’s biceps so tight that Yelena let out a gasp of pain as well. “I don’t know! My side—it’s on fricking fire—fuck.” Arching her back, Kate cried out with renewed agony. There was silver inside her—silver that had forced her wolf into retreat out of self-preservation. But now, her wolf was awake—her wolf was awake, and it was screaming.

    Ripping off the blanket, Yelena grabbed both sides of the flannel and tore them apart, buttons flying everywhere. With care but with a franticness borne of Kate’s whimpers and trembling, Yelena removed the bandage and the gauze pad.

    “The veins,” Yelena gasped, her words barely audible to Kate over the roaring in her ears. Sweat broke out all over Kate’s body as she forced herself to look down. The black lines that had barely been visible before now seemed impossibly prominent. As though she acted on instinct, Yelena pushed both palms against Kate’s side and stomach, covering the veins with her fingers. “No—no. No! They were getting smaller! You were healing!”

    “My wolf,” Kate whispered, uncomfortably aware of Yelena’s touch. The full moon was over a week away—it was over a week away. Her wolf had been asleep—“I don’t know.”

    Throwing back her head again, Kate screamed as the silver dug itself deeper into her flesh, her teeth growing long and gnarly inside her mouth. Half-transformed.

    “Last time—” Yelena said, gasping in relief as Kate’s hands fell back onto the mattress.

    Kate’s nails turned to claws, ripping the sheets to shreds as she dug them in. “Kate!” Yelena’s hands left the veins, and a stronger surge of blinding pain curled itself around Kate’s heart and mind.

    Her half-shifted hands barely managed to catch Yelena’s wrists. This time, when she pulled Yelena’s palms back to the pain, she didn’t bother avoiding the bullet hole. As soon as Yelena’s hand dug into the wound, Kate howled, her pupils dilating and her wolf taking control over her body.

    “Kate!” Yelena shouted, but her words no longer penetrated the haze that overtook Kate’s mind. The wolf could only feel her touch—the wolf could only taste a mixture of blood and silver in the air—the wolf could only smell the sweet highlights of a familiar scent—the wolf could only see the darkness of the night—the wolf could hear nothing but the sweet whispers of her moon.

    I’m here. You’re here.

    “Stop,” a voice said a million miles away. It was her moon, whispering to her. “Stop hurting yourself.”

    The silver burned under her skin, lighting up her senses and razing everything to the ground. The wolf remembered. Kate did not remember, but the wolf remembered. The wolf remembered the bullet hitting Yelena’s side and Natasha’s shoulder. The wolf remembered the hunter aiming at Yelena’s head. The wolf remembered so that Kate did not have to.

    “Stop,” her moon said again, her words a thousand times sweeter than the satisfaction of the hunt. Drawing blood would never be the same again. Drawing blood for her moon had dwarfed all other kills. “This is not what we discussed.” The wolf howled, basking in the warmth of her moon’s love. “Don’t do this to me. Don’t leave me.”

    The silver liked to overwhelm its victims, and Kate was dying. The wolf could feel her agony —their agony. But the wolf was strong, and her moon’s love was even stronger. The wolf would not deny Kate their strength.

    ##

    January 6th, 2017 | 01:15 A.M.

    Gunshots. Screams. The gurgling of a throat filling with blood.

    The smell of pine needles and wet earth permeated the air. It had snowed not too long ago— or had it? Snow never failed to make her smile. Once upon a time, she’d loved nothing more than building snowmen with her father. Back then, she’d been certain of his love. Now that he was dead, she wasn’t so sure—he never met the wolf.

    Warmth suffused her body—and the touch of a hand demanded her attention. When she drew her next breath, she became aware of a weight on her chest, barely restricting her movement. She took another. Another breath, another gurgling scream.

    But no, that wasn’t right, was it?

    Opening her eyes, Kate found herself staring into the darkness. The smell of pine needles and wet earth vanished, replaced with the familiar smell of someone’s hair. Slowly moving her hands, she became aware that her left arm was pinned beneath a body.

    Blinking into the pitch black, Kate moved her right hand under the blanket. Her side didn’t hurt, and she realized why when she moved her fingers across it. The bullet wound was gone. Left behind was nothing but a small circle of raised skin. Few things left permanent marks on a werewolf’s body. The bite was one of them; silver was another.

    “Damn,” Kate whispered to herself, utterly confused. The last thing she remembered before passing out was arguing with Yelena about her involvement in fights with hunters. The fight had triggered a blinding pain in her side, and that was it. There was nothing else.

    “Damn is right.”

    With a slight flinch, Kate glanced to her right, her wolf eyes catching Natasha’s silhouette in the darkness. The woman leaned back in a comfortable chair that hadn’t been in the room earlier. Having been pulled from her thoughts, Kate realized that the weight on her chest was Yelena’s head and that Yelena’s hand rested on her sternum. Judging by the soft inhales and exhales, Yelena was fast asleep.

    “Why are you up?” Kate whispered, slipping her right hand out from under the blanket. She didn’t dare move or touch Yelena for fear of disturbing her.

    “Can’t say no to my kid sister,” Natasha explained, tilting her head to the side and painting a silly smile onto her lips. The tight lines around her eyes gave away her worry—more and more, Kate learned to see the truth behind the jokes. “Who was freaking out about your screaming and your half-transformation, by the way. Not to say that I wasn’t a bit concerned myself, Jack.”

    “Half-transformation?” Kate asked, racking her brain for the right piece of information.

    With a gentle sigh, Natasha shrugged.

    “I was checking on our guests when I heard your… vocalizations,” Natasha revealed, her eyes moving from Kate to Yelena. The silly smile slipped from her lips as she recounted what Kate could not remember. “When I got up here, I found Yelena with her hands on your bloody stomach. And there was a lot more fur on you than normal.” Running a hand through her hair, Natasha gently furrowed her brows. “I haven’t seen a lot of half-transformations in my life.”

    “I’ve never half-transformed,” Kate whispered, finally giving into her wolf’s desire to touch Yelena. Softly, so as not to startle her, Kate placed her right palm over the back of Yelena’s hand on her sternum. “I’ve seen pictures.” Of course, the diagrams and cross-sections of werewolf anatomy her parents showed her during hunter training probably didn’t reflect reality all that much. “I didn’t hurt either of you, did I?”

    “No,” Natasha replied, placing her left elbow on the armrest of the comfortable chair before resting her cheek on her fist. “To be honest with you, it looked more like you were hurting yourself.” Pursing her lips, she studied Kate’s face and upper chest. “What triggered that shift, do you think? You were doing fine when I left you. The liquid silver in your system was stubborn, but it was slowly working its way out.”

    “I don’t know.” Kate moved her eyes back to the ceiling, thinking back to the few minutes before she’d lost consciousness. “I guess… fighting with Yelena woke up my wolf. She was angry with me for leaving the car and kept telling me I needed to let her do all the scary, dangerous stuff.”

    “That sounds like her,” Natasha whispered fondly. Her words were barely audible, and if Kate’s wolf powers hadn’t been restored, she would have missed the comment entirely. “I guess waking up your wolf isn’t the worst outcome that could have happened. At least now your wound is all healed up.” Kate’s eyes returned to Natasha’s face, but her friend’s gaze had moved elsewhere. “Interesting.”

    “What is?” Kate asked, feeling Yelena’s hand twitch under her own.

    “Nothing,” Natasha replied, lifting her cheek off her fist before returning to her relaxed pose from earlier. “We should think about our next move now that you’re feeling better.” Folding her hands over her stomach, Natasha closed her eyes and crossed her ankles. “We’ll be done with our guests soon.”

    “Really?” Kate asked while her ears registered the slight elevation of Yelena’s heartbeat. “Yes,” Natasha confirmed, just as Yelena let out a slow exhale that drifted across the back of Kate’s hand. With a careful squeeze, Kate let her know that she was awake and that she was fine. “Give it one or two more days, and we’ll have what we need.”

    “Always so optimistic,” Yelena drawled, her voice thick with residual sleepiness.

    “One of us has to be,” Natasha said, drumming her fingers on her stomach.

    With a hum, Yelena pushed herself up on her right hand. When she pulled back her left from under Kate’s fingers, something sharp and painful jabbed Kate’s heart. A second later, Yelena touched Kate’s stomach as though to reassure herself that her skin remained closed. The sweet gesture mended some of the damage done to Kate’s chest.

    “All healed up,” Kate whispered, dropping her right hand awkwardly back to the mattress.

    Yelena’s face seemed impassive, but Kate knew better. Even still, she couldn’t stop herself. “I’m sorry I bled all over you—Natasha said your hands were on the wound when it closed. I know that, uh, can be a bit gruesome. You know… with werewolf wounds. They don’t pull any punches. I hope the snapping stitches didn’t sting.”

    For a long, agonizing moment, Yelena said nothing. Her eyes were fixed on the blanket, right above where her hand lingered on Kate’s stomach. If Kate didn’t know better, she’d have thought Yelena could see right through it.

    “Does it still hurt?” Yelena asked, smoothing her thumb across the scar tissue.

    Swallowing thickly, Kate wished Natasha wasn’t sitting quite so close to them. Clearing her throat, she hoped her voice wouldn’t crack. “I told you,” Kate replied, moving her hand under the blanket to cover Yelena’s fingers once more. “I’m all healed up. No more pain.” Yelena pulled back her hand, twisting the knife of confusion in Kate’s heart in a new direction. Yelena had never pulled away from her touch before—not like this. Not since the cabin. “You don’t have to worry.”

    After a sharp, derisive snap of her tongue, Yelena was out of bed and on her feet.

    “Of course,” she said, lingering by the window. “You keep saying this.” Curling her right hand into a fist, Yelena nodded to herself. The lack of light made her features hard to read, but Kate didn’t know what to say or what to ask to make this better. “No need to worry. In that case, we will reconvene in the morning. Now that you are well, we can discuss our options.”

    Placing her hands on her waist, Yelena nodded again. Then, she left the bedroom before Kate could change the outcome of this brief interaction. Less than a minute ago, Yelena had been curled up against her as though to reassure herself that Kate was fine. And now? Blinking into the darkness, Kate opened her mouth and said—nothing.

    “Lovely,” Natasha finally commented, crossing her arms over her chest.

    “You’re not leaving?” Kate asked, slumping back against her pillow. What was she missing? Did Yelena say something to her before she passed out? Did she say something to Yelena? If her last few interactions with Yelena had been a test of some kind, Kate was failing. Badly. “Like I said,” Natasha concluded, closing her eyes as she lifted her right shoulder in a shrug. “I’m not very good at saying no to my kid sister.” With a sigh, Kate turned onto her side, her eyes fixed on her friend’s face. Letting out a sigh, Kate opened her mouth again. “Don’t ask me, Jack.”

    “Okay,” Kate grumbled, mouth pulling into a pout. “Fine.”

    Natasha shook her head with a chuckle, and Kate closed her eyes.

    Drifting off did not come easily to Kate, who kept imagining the weird expression on Yelena’s face right before she left the bedroom. There had been something sad about it; there had been something angry about it; there had been something—something tender about it. As Kate clutched the duvet to her chest, she thought of those moments before she passed out. She thought of Yelena’s fury, of Yelena’s hurt, and of Yelena’s worry. Whispered words flowed into her mind. I’m here. You’re here.

    Who are you, and who am I, she thought. Who are you, and why is your voice in my head?

    But sleep was faster than the answer, so Kate drifted off to the quiet, even sounds of Natasha’s breathing.

    ##

    January 6th, 2017 | 08:23 A.M.

    Kate’s favorite brand of cereal sat on the countertop of the small kitchen that housed little more than a stovetop, an oven, and a sink. The tiny refrigerator sat in a corner of the room, wedged between the small space between the door and the wall. The kitchen had a window at eye level, but Kate could see little more than a brick wall across from it.

    “You’re up,” Yelena said, startling Kate from her morning musings. “Good. Natasha and I are in the study.” Unlike Kate, who wore an unbloodied hoodie and a pair of clean sweatpants, Yelena was clad from neck to toe in black tactical gear. The look reminded Kate of their attack on the hunters, but it wasn’t the same. This tactical suit seemed somehow sharper— deadlier. “Come on.”

    “But—” Kate started, gesturing at the cereal. “Hungry.” Yelena pursed her lips, wilting any hope that Kate might have had about their weird non-fight being over. After her shower, Kate had found a bloody flannel in the bathroom’s trashcan. The buttons had nearly all been ripped off, sparking a few more flimsy memories in Kate’s mind. “I’ll be quick. I’ll pour the milk straight into the bag and slurp it while we talk.”

    Yelena never blinked. “That is not for you,” she lied, grabbing Kate’s right bicep and tugging on it. When Kate didn’t budge—her werewolf strength having been returned—Yelena scowled, and Kate pouted harder.

    “But it’s my favorite.”

    “You are worse than Lucky,” Yelena grumbled, sparking a mixture of longing and regret in Kate’s chest. Things had been easier in so many ways back then. Without further argument, Yelena released Kate’s arm and removed her scowling face from the kitchen while Kate looked after her longingly.

    “I’ll be there in five minutes,” Kate called after her seconds before she touched her own bicep in mild confusion. A tingling remained in the place where Yelena had touched her, though she had no idea why. She was used to her heart speeding up whenever Yelena smiled at her, but she didn’t know how to feel about this new side effect.

    With a big bowl of cereal and a spoon, Kate left the kitchen in search of the study. Lifting her nose, she sniffed the air until she found the room she was looking for. Kate had no idea why a house this small had to have a study, but she was beyond questioning her friends’ safe houses at this point.

    When she entered, Kate spotted Natasha sitting behind a desk while Yelena stood at the window. It was a similar setup to their last discussion of upcoming plans, although this time, Kate felt much closer to both women—not just Yelena.

    “What’s up?” Kate asked, kicking the study door closed behind her. Since there was only one chair in the room, which Natasha currently occupied, Kate looked around for an appropriate place to sit. When she spotted the bookshelf lining the wall facing the desk, she plonked down in front of it. Holding her bowl and spoon far away from her body to avoid spillage, she quickly crossed her legs to get more comfortable. “Did something happen?”

    “Charlotte Kelly has revealed the full extent to which you have been compromised,” Yelena said, never turning towards Kate as she spoke. From where Kate sat, she could barely make out Yelena’s reflection in the window. While Yelena’s hands were crossed behind her back, her feet lined up perfectly with her shoulders. Her stance was strong—disciplined—

    untouchable.

    “Oh?” Kate asked, holding her breath as she lowered the bowl to her lap. Heartbeat quickening, she moved her eyes from Yelena’s back to Natasha’s face. Both were equally unreadable. “What did she say?”

    Natasha’s hands lay motionless on the desk. When she spoke, she leaned forward, her palms sliding across the smooth, wooden surface. When she stopped, they curled into fists. “Almost everyone who knew about you is dead.” Natasha’s mouth pulled into a cheerless smile.

    “Congratulations, Kate. You get to go home soon.”

    Kate’s mouth dropped open as a pit opened up in her stomach, and her mind began racing at a hundred miles a minute. “Almost?”

    “We killed nineteen hunters in the forest,” Yelena explained, carefully stretching first her left and then her right shoulder without once losing her powerful posture. “Among the nineteen were Kelly and Trask’s three lieutenants. The other sixteen were higher-level hunters. A mix of Kelly’s and your mother’s employees.”

    “Okay,” Kate said, immediately wondering who was dead. She knew pretty much everyone at Bishop Security—who would be missing the next time she went there? Her stomach twisted itself into knots of indecision. Perhaps she should feel guilty that they were in the ground instead of at their desks. Would her mother miss them? Or would she be more relieved that Kate was alive, even though some of her former colleagues were not? And what of Natasha and Yelena? “And that means…”

    “You said it yourself,” Natasha continued, unclenching her fists before folding her hands on the table. “Hunters are distrustful by nature. They question everything—that’s why they followed you. That’s why they were there when you shifted.” Natasha shrugged, leaning back in her chair. “But it also means they’re careful about sharing information. Never know who the enemy might be when the enemy can look like you, right?”

    “Yeah… right,” Kate mumbled to herself, staring down into her bowl of progressively soggier cereal. This was real. Most of the hunters who knew about her were gone. She could return to her life; she could go back to university; she could see her friends and her mom again; she could—“What about you guys?”

    “What about us?” Natasha asked, casually crossing her arms over her chest.

    “Really?” Furrowing her brows, Kate pulled on the right drawstring of her hoodie to give herself something to do. When Natasha looked at Yelena’s back, Kate scowled. Unbelievable. “I’m not leaving until you’re both safe.”

    “Look, Kate,” Natasha said, lifting both her hands in a placating gesture.

    “I’m not looking at anything!” Kate replied hotly, feeling her wolf blood sing with something altogether too dangerous to contain. Maybe it would have been a stretch to call Yelena and Natasha pack, considering just how little time they’d spent together, yet her wolf didn’t seem to care. It took every bit of willpower she had not to take a step forward and grab Yelena’s bicep to make her turn around and face the truth. “You’re my friends; friends don’t let friends fight secret paramilitary organizations by themselves!”

    “We are not friends.” Yelena’s hands stayed behind her back.     “You’re saying that to make me go away,” Kate snarled, dropping her arms and curling her hands into fists. “But it’s not going to work.” Leaning further in, Kate took a deep and calming breath that was full of Yelena’s natural scent. Unlike Kate, she seemed to have no qualms about grabbing Yelena’s bicep and pulling her away. “I don’t want to put Kate in danger, either, but this is not how we discussed we’d go about it. If you can’t deal with the way you feel about her, that’s your business.”

    Her words ping-ponged through Kate’s brain, bouncing off all the important memories from the previous month, taking much firmer root than Yelena’s insults. If you can’t deal with the way you feel about her, that’s your business. With the way Yelena felt what about her?

    Unbidden, Kate’s heart skipped a few beats, even though she should have been insulted by Yelena’s obvious lack of faith in her.

    “Shut up,” Yelena grumbled, tugging her arm free of Natasha’s grip.

    “You shut up,” Natasha replied, rolling her eyes. “Brat.”

    “Nuisance,” Yelena whispered under her breath, scowling at her sister. Her next words were in Russian. They were quick, and they sounded plenty angry, even though Kate had no idea what she was saying. Natasha replied in kind, leaving Kate to ponder the inevitable spiral they kept finding themselves in. Yelena barked back another string of Russian, most of which Kate surmised to be curse words.

    Shaking her head, Kate slumped back to the ground and picked up her cereal.

    “How do we keep getting here?” she asked, sticking a spoonful of soggy breakfast into her mouth.

    “What?” Yelena asked, her angry mask slipping—probably against her will.

    “I said: How do we keep getting here?” Kate repeated after swallowing, pulling her legs back until she sat cross-legged once more. “You make plans without me; I say I want to help; one or both of you say it’s a bad idea; I insist I want to help; you list all the reasons why it’s not going to work, and then you both agree to let me come with you anyway.     You know I want you to be safe. We’ve talked about this. We both know we’re not changing our minds, so why do we keep having this argument?”

    With a hum and a chuckle, Natasha also slumped down on the ground.

    “Jack’s got a point,” she said, leaning back on her hands. “Why do you keep having this argument?”

    Pursing her lips, Yelena finally uncrossed her hands from behind her back.

    “Because we are highly trained, and Kate Bishop is not,” Yelena said, although her words sounded almost rehearsed. Shaking her head, she also sat down, although she pulled her knees to her chest and wrapped her arms around them. “I have never lost a friend. I have lost a sister.” Staring at her feet, Yelena shrugged. “I do not like losing.”

    “Hug?” she asked, hating the smell of despair that suddenly permeated the air. In a way, she almost preferred the Yelena who tried to insult her. When Yelena looked at her, Kate knew her offer had been accepted. Sitting up on her knees, she wrapped her arms around Yelena’s shoulders and embraced her tightly.

    “You know,” Natasha said while Kate smushed her cheek against Yelena’s shoulder and snuggled in, squeezing her eyes shut. “You won’t lose me again.” In all the time they’d traveled together, Kate had never heard her sound quite so sincere. “We’re smarter this time. And we’re stronger this time.” Natasha’s hand settled on Kate’s upper back. “Especially the three of us. Together.”

    With a sigh, Yelena returned Kate’s hug.

    “I suppose you are not pathetic, and you are not a burden,” Yelena hedged to Kate, squeezing her sides and smushing her own face against Kate’s shoulder. The feeling sent tingles all along Kate’s spine and into her toes. Had Kate been a werecat of some kind, she would have started purring. “I’m sorry.”

    “Thanks,” Kate replied, fighting the urge to kiss Yelena’s neck. “Told you I didn’t believe you.”

    “Don’t be smug,” Yelena said, her voice muffled against Kate’s hoodie as she pinched the skin of Kate’s waist.

    “I think Jack’s entitled to a bit of smugness,” Natasha interjected, pulling back from them to get back to her feet. Walking over to the desk, she returned to her earlier position. “We are taking down the Red Room, by the way. We just need a better plan than running right at them with a pair of guns and a can-do attitude.”

    “If you ask me, I think it’s a good start,” Kate said, forcing her face to remove itself from Yelena’s warm, sweet-smelling neck. The words were still bouncing back and forth in her brain, hitting inconvenient spots and causing her wolf to scrape and scrabble at its walls. To her infinite delight, Yelena did not pull away from her.

    “It’s too predictable,” Natasha continued, leaning back in her comfortable desk chair. With her elbows on the armrests, she steepled her fingers together in front of her chest, making her look vaguely ridiculous and one hundred percent adorable.

    “Plus, Dreykov will have changed security protocols and widow patrols by now,” Yelena said, stretching her legs and placing her hands on her knees before bowing her back. She, too, looked very adorable as she spread her fingers, especially for an assassin in her tactical gear. “We need someone with more information. Someone who knows more about Dreykov’s dealings than we do.”

    With a hum, Kate went back to her conversation with Yelena and Natasha in the farmhouse kitchen a few weeks ago. When they first started planning to take down the hunters who wanted her dead, Kate had thought it inconceivable that any maneuver on their part would work. Now that the hunters were all but dealt with, the Red Room seemed a hundred times worse, making Kate feel sheepish about how she reacted.

    “Well, they won’t be expecting a ferocious predator,” Kate said, removing her arm from around Yelena so she could sit and then stand up. Flexing her left bicep, Kate ignored the lingering feelings of guilt about her last run-in with Red Room soldiers. It was for Yelena and Natasha. It was for Yelena and Natasha. If she didn’t think about how their flesh felt as it tore under her claws, then she couldn’t beat herself up over it. “We’ve got the werewolfantage!” “They probably had video surveillance at the hangar,” Yelena said, looking up at Kate from her position on the ground.

    “Yeah, they definitely know who you are,” Natasha added with a shrug, sounding unapologetic as Kate’s bravado deflated like a popped balloon. “Also, I never want you to use that word around me again.”

    “Aw,” Kate muttered, dropping her shoulders and arms and kicking ineffectually at the ground.

    Somehow, Kate didn’t knock over the cereal bowl in the study. As far as achievements went, this one ranked pretty high—mainly because Kate ended up slumping back on the ground with too much petulance to care about where her butt ended up.

    As she descended the stairs to the basement, her soggy cereal was the furthest thing from her mind.

With both of them otherwise engaged, Kate wanted to see for herself what the two had been doing with their visitors while she’d been recovering.

    Even halfway down, she could see several metal bars.

    “Cage,” she muttered to herself, curling her hands into fists before stuffing them into the big front pocket of her hoodie. “Fun.” Nervously licking her lips, she exhaled through her nose and attempted not to think about the last time she tried to lock herself away. At least this safe house came with a dedicated holding cell instead of an unsuspecting laundry room. Although Kate wondered if even these bars would be enough to stop her wolf.

    Ten more days, and she’d have to make another decision. Ten more days and the moon would once again demand her fealty. Looking at her stomach, Kate pursed her lips. Silver poisoning should have prevented her wolf from reemerging so soon. Stranger still, she should not be feeling this strong just yet. Ten more days, yet she felt like she could shift and change any minute now.

She might have expected to see Kelly and Trask’s emaciated and tortured bodies tied to chairs.     “It is called the future of warfare, actually,” a voice said seconds after Kate smelled the fresh scent of body wash and shampoo drifting through the air. Or until their minds were too broken to keep resisting. I am unsure. It is hard to tell.”

If she turned into a whimpering whelp every time they had to do something horrible, she’d never be able to follow them to the ends of the Earth. You only just started painting your own trail… How far will it reach if you continue to travel with us? Maybe Yelena wouldn’t have asked her that question back then if she’d known. Very, very old,” Yelena continued with an imperceptible smirk, even though her sister wasn’t there to hear her needling. “She has not been with the Red Room for many years. If her new government wanted to use this chemical on her, I am sure she would be able to resist it on her own, but she has not been trained for it. I have.” Kate didn’t need to ask how training to resist a chemical might work. “It is still not a pleasant experience, but I would not divulge information to the enemy.”

Kate had seen Yelena in tactical gear only recently, but even then, she’d retained parts of herself. Parts Kate was sure the Red Room would have seen eradicated. Imagining Yelena strapped to a table made Kate want to throw up.

Slowly, tentatively, she touched Yelena’s shoulder. “I can’t imagine.” When Yelena lifted her left hand to cover Kate’s fingers, a new warmth flooded Kate’s chest. It was an inappropriate reaction for where they were standing, yet Kate couldn’t bring herself to break their connection. “What will happen to Kelly and Trask now that you have all the information you need?”

    “We will kill them, of course,” she said without preamble and without remorse. Ripping off the band-aid, perhaps, on what she perceived to be Kate’s naïve and gentle soul. “They know too much about you; they know too much about us. It would be irresponsible to let them go.” She tugged Kate’s hand off her shoulder. But she is.” Yelena squeezed Kate’s fingers. It felt like an apology. “We do not have the resources to care for them until it is safe for Natasha to return.”

    “I understand,” Kate said, squeezing Yelena’s fingers right back. Taking a step closer, Kate ached to wrap Yelena in her arms and hold her until the guilt went away. “I know what they do. When Yelena looked up at her, there was something hidden behind her gaze. “My parents are killers. I was supposed to be a killer, too. So, I get it. I get that they’re dangerous. I get it.”

Ten more days, and already… A part of her wanted to howl, to growl, to curl up in Yelena’s lap. Kate didn’t realize she was moving until her forehead brushed against Yelena’s.

    “Why are you apologizing?” Yelena asked, nudging their noses together. Yelena’s mouth was so close—Yelena’s lips always looked so soft. Even when they delivered cutting remarks, blades glittering in the light of her eyes, they never lost their softness. “Yelena, you—you’re—”
